with herdr present, install output and hooks are byte-identical to today. | R13 [N] Beside Herdr, a named caller with Herdr env uses the Herdr command group's store, the default state dir is not created when Herdr is configured or on PATH, and doctor flags two live stores for one user. | R14 [N] A second unrelated live process claiming a held name is refused with M7; once the holder exits or its pid is reused the name binds at once; subagents and HERDR_THREADS_SEAT_READONLY=1 bindings are admitted beside a live holder. | R15 [N] A readonly binding of a named seat reads and sends normally but never ACKs receipt-bearing rows on display or in turn hooks, while a concurrent non-readonly binding's display ACKs. | R16 [N] Send, inbox, ACK and hook delivery work between two named seats and between a named seat and a Herdr pane seat through one daemon, and handoff from a named seat into a Herdr pane works with the endpoint resolved lazily. | R17 [N] A name inherited from a host server's environment (Herdr) is refused with M6 and never bound, while a name set per command inside a pane is honoured. | R18 [N] Named-seat store invariants hold: a named seat missing from an enumeration stays resolved; RetireReason::Closed is refused; no cross-kind rebind in either direction; the named host never satisfies wait:host; an unresolved named seat is reattached by the next resolve of its name (cooperative_named_seat); one unresolved named row does not silence liveness of other named bindings. | R19 [N] The second-locator notice M9 fires on the first binding from a second locator only, survives daemon restart, and seat inspect lists bindings by locator. | R20 [N] No-wake reporting for named seats: one wake=no_wake per (sender, seat) across restart and mod-channel flap; hooks-only Claude classifies None and live-mod Claude reports no block; overdue line ends 'wake=no_wake offline' (M19) once bindings end while pane-seat bytes are unchanged; one recipient_unavailable per offline episode for ACK/p0 sends; deadlines yield only the sender's overdue line; seat threshold on a named seat records the value and prints the inert-threshold line M12. | R21 [N] seat retire --self retires a named seat, lists abandoned items, replicates as Operator, records cooperative_self_retire, is refused for pane seats (M20), subagents and while another connected top-level binding exists without --force (M18), replays idempotently; human seat retire --operator also retires; reusing the name mints a new seat. | R22 [N] Restore of a seat with display_name comes back unresolved on the named host, or stays for the operator when a live seat holds that name. | R23 [N] Cross-node addressing: reviewer@B addresses and displays across nodes, and an old-node fixture decodes display_name. | R24 [N] thread show, delivery from headers and seat inspect render the seat name (workspace and tab '-', name shown once; - finding-source · {"version": 1, "source": "roast-code/round-1", "artifact": "2026-11-01-environments-named-seats-herdr-app-roast-pr-1.md", "kind": "roast", "verdict": "Should-fix (39 confirmed) [low coverage] [review readiness incomplete]", "occurrences": [{"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 1, "key": "[Should-fix] src/store/apply.rs:985", "text": "[Should-fix] src/store/apply.rs:985 — On a node with more than one non-named host kind, a parked unnamed own `seat.created` keeps PARKED_READY true while its materializer keeps re-parking it, so `apply_pass` spends every pass re-attempting it, never reaches log heads, and reports `has_more` forever. [lanes: data-migrations]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: PARKED_READY narrowed by the diff to `kind<>'named'` (apply.rs:985) while `restore_own_seat` (src/store/seats/publication.rs ~286-310) now returns HostUnavailable when `count(DISTINCT kind)<=1` fails; facts/seat.rs:62-69 maps that to `Wait{on:Host}`; `reattempt_parked` (apply.rs:1005-1017) loops `next_reattemptable`, which ignores `run.attempted`, until `run.exhausted()`, then returns true so `apply_pass` breaks before `next_ready_head`; workers.rs:1386 re-hints on `has_more` with no backoff. Not executed; chain read from source by all three seats.\n  fix-shape hint: make PARKED_READY mirror the materializer's single-kind condition (or have `next_reattemptable` skip rows in `run.attempted`), plus the spike test the finding names (two-kind store, own unnamed seat.created, two passes, second `has_more=false`)."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 2, "key": "[Should-fix] src/store/seats/publication.rs:245", "text": "[Should-fix] src/store/seats/publication.rs:245 — Own-log restore after a wipe brings a retired named seat back as a live `unresolved` seat holding the name, so the retired seat takes the name, its live successor is held nameless, and the next resolve of the name reattaches the retired seat. [lanes: data-migrations]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `restore_own_seat` (publication.rs ~239-290) inserts every own `seat.created` as `state='unresolved'` and never looks for a later `seat.retired`; `materialize_seat_retired` begins `if m.local_origin { return Ok(()); }`; `named_seat_apply` (resolution.rs ~740-760) selects `name=?2 AND state<>'retired'` and calls `reattach_named_seat` on an unresolved row. Design lines 418-419 say \"Retire ends continuity for good\". tests/store/named_restore.rs has no retired-before-restore case (its `retired` assertions at 309/338 follow a retire made after the restore).\n  fix-shape hint: during own-log replay, let a local-origin `seat.retired` retire the restored row (or have restore skip a `seat.created` with a later own `seat.retired`), and add the A-created/A-retired/B-created replay test."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 3, "key": "[Should-fix] src/host/hc/mod.rs:877; src/host/hc/mod.rs:878", "text": "[Should-fix] src/host/hc/mod.rs:877; src/host/hc/mod.rs:878 — HcHost::enumerate_targets turns a failed core capture (socket gone, refused, decode error) into Ok with complete:false instead of Err, so observation takes the durable-invalidation path that ends every open binding on the core, while Health reports a causeless PartialEnumeration and reachability is marked up. [lanes: premortem, observability]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: mod.rs:877-878 `let Some(capture) = self.capture() else { return Ok(Self::unverified(..)) }`; `capture()` (~328) collapses every failure via `.ok()?`; `unverified()` sets `complete:false`. reconcile.rs `observe_and_publish`: `Err(_)` → HostUnavailable (Frozen) but `!complete` → PartialEnumeration; `is_unavailability` (ports.rs:971) excludes it; hc is Closing so `invalidate_host_observation` runs and `mark_seat_unresolved` (observation.rs ~2021-2085) ends open bindings. workers.rs ~2545 calls `mark_up` for Invalidated. Design lines 706-709 and mod.rs:36/870 say a failed hello \"closes nothing\"; the native Herdr path returns Err and freezes. Not executed.\n  fix-shape hint: return Err (HostUnavailable, with the capture error as cause) from `enumerate_targets` when `capture()` fails, keep CompleteUnverified for the readable-but-unverified case, and add an observe_and_publish test for a refused hc socket."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 4, "key": "[Should-fix] src/service/turn_state.rs:35", "text": "[Should-fix] src/service/turn_state.rs:35 — The turn-state key is seat plus seat generation, not a per-binding identity, so two connected top-level bindings of one seat share one entry and one binding's Stop marks the pane idle while the other is mid-turn, and the wake gate then types into a running session. [lanes: correctness]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `TurnKey{seat, generation}`; dispatch.rs:1219 builds it from `claim.binding_generation`, which cooperative.rs:645 sets to `mapping.generation` and cooperative.rs:1050 says check-ins never bump; `top_level_generations` (named_seats.rs:41) returns equal values for every binding; named.rs:271-299 reduces \"every live top-level binding idle\" to one shared entry; `record(Idle)` always overwrites. SA16 admits several top-level bindings per seat; design D21 says turn state is \"per binding\"; tests/service/turn_state.rs uses a single key.\n  fix-shape hint: key the book on a per-binding identity (binding id or the hook's session id) and make the gate require every binding's entry Idle; add a two-binding test."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 5, "key": "[Should-fix] src/cli/mod.rs:3940", "text": "[Should-fix] src/cli/mod.rs:3940 — The CLI's seat lookup for a lone herdr-core Terminal site is not host-scoped: it queries by bare tN with host None, so two attached cores that both own a seated t1 make every seat-acting CLI command in either terminal fail with 'pane t1 maps to more than one seat', and when only the other core's t1 has a seat the lookup returns that foreign seat. [lanes: correctness]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: run() (mod.rs ~349-357) sets caller_pane to the bare tN; SelfMarker/SeatDefault/derive_caller arms (~3716, ~3741, ~4108) call `located_pane_seat` → `pane_seat` → `pane_seat_on(.., None, ..)` (3883); store/queries.rs:1642 applies the host filter only `if let Some(host)`. `site_capability::seats_host` (site_capability.rs:51) already returns the core socket for a Terminal but only `named_site_seat` (3989) and hook.rs:1583 use it. herdr-core ids are per core and start at t1.\n  fix-shape hint: pass `seats_host(site)` into the Terminal path's seat lookup (use `pane_seat_on(.., Some(host), ..)`), and add a two-core CLI caller test."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 6, "key": "[Should-fix] src/service/locate.rs:376", "text": "[Should-fix] src/service/locate.rs:376 — LocateCaller's terminal candidate and the inherited-name check ignore the candidate's socket and take the first attached core answering for tN, so with two attached cores a caller in the second core's terminal is compared against the wrong core's root (cached NotAncestor refusal or wrong environ read). [lanes: correctness, premortem, simplicity-design]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `candidate_root` matches `LocateCandidate::Terminal { id, .. }` (socket dropped; protocol/locate.rs:15 carries it), loops every `answers_terminal_ids()` host and returns the first `Ok(Some(pid))`; dispatch.locate_caller passes all_for_reads unfiltered; `inherited_name_host` (:189) `.find`s the first terminal host likewise. Design line 726 and K4 (line 64) treat two cores both owning t1 as the designed case; `HostSessions::by_locator` exists. NotAncestor is cached until the host-set epoch changes. tests/service/locate.rs has no two-core case.\n  fix-shape hint: select the host by the candidate's socket via `by_locator` (and carry the socket in the inherited-name hint), falling back to the loop only when no socket is known."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 7, "key": "[Should-fix] src/cli/caller_location.rs:143", "text": "[Should-fix] src/cli/caller_location.rs:143 — The diff makes every CLI child read HC_TERMINAL_ID/HC_TERMINAL_SOCKET from the process environment, but the test scrub never removes them, so the suite's result depends on whether the runner sits in a herdr-core terminal: 54 to 90 of 1309 tests fail there, against the two zsh-absence failures in a clean environment. [lanes: test-isolation-environment]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `CallerEnv::from_lookup` chains HC_TERMINAL_ID_ENV/HC_TERMINAL_SOCKET_ENV (caller_location.rs:143, 185-186, 217-218); `SCRUBBED_PREFIXES = [\"HERDR_\", \"CLAUDE\", \"CODEX\", \"ZDOTDIR\"]` (src/test_support/isolation.rs:15) unchanged by the diff, whose doc promises the suite behaves the same inside a pane or harness session; base `git grep HC_TERMINAL` over src is empty. Seats verified the mechanism statically; the 54/90 failure counts are the scout's observed runs, not re-run by the panel.\n  fix-shape hint: add `HC_` (and TMUX/TMUX_PANE, see the companion Nit) to the scrub in test_support, not per test."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 8, "key": "[Should-fix] src/host/named/tmux.rs:221", "text": "[Should-fix] src/host/named/tmux.rs:221 — When the harness is itself the tmux pane process, the binding is anchored on the short-lived hook process, so the verified binding is pruned at once and the seat silently has no tmux wake. [lanes: correctness]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `verify_walk` returns `LivenessFingerprint::of(&below.unwrap_or(record))`, the process one step below pane_pid on the walk from the subject; the subject is `peer.pid()` from dispatch.rs ~1992 (the connecting herdr-threads hook/CLI process). With `tmux new-session 'claude'` the pane process is the harness, so `below` is the hook child, which exits; `live_bindings`/`wake_armed`/`target_wake_capability` prune on `anchor.is_alive()`. Design ~561 walks \"from the binding's fingerprint pid\". The integration test runs under a long-lived runner.sh in a shell, so it never hits this shape.\n  fix-shape hint: anchor on the harness (the binding's own fingerprint, or the first non-caller process below pane_pid), and add a test where the pane command is the harness itself."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 9, "key": "[Should-fix] src/host/hc/mod.rs:320", "text": "[Should-fix] src/host/hc/mod.rs:320 — HcHost::resume_after_epoch is an inherent method, not a HostPort override, so the daemon's `port.resume_after_epoch(..)` on `Arc<dyn HostPort>` runs the trait's no-op default and HcHost's version has no caller. [lanes: simplicity-design]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `pub fn resume_after_epoch` sits in `impl HcHost` (mod.rs:320); `impl HostPort for HcHost` (from 697) has no override; ports.rs:4394 default is `{}`; host_sessions.rs:740/816 call it on the factory's `Arc<dyn HostPort>`; main.rs:73 builds `Arc::new(HcHost::new(..))` with no wrapper; epoch starts at 1 (mod.rs:279). NamedHost (named.rs:465) and NativeCli (native.rs:1835) put it in the trait impl. No test calls the HcHost method.\n  fix-shape hint: move the method into `impl HostPort for HcHost` (or add a trait forwarder like NativeCli) and add a restart-epoch test."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 10, "key": "[Should-fix] src/cli/caller_guard.rs:98", "text": "[Should-fix] src/cli/caller_guard.rs:98 — A verified terminal in a two-family environment gets no seat: the CLI ends with neither pane nor site and hooks return an empty outcome, so an agent in an hc terminal of a core autostarted from a Herdr pane cannot use messaging even though LocateCaller identified it correctly. [lanes: correctness]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived (refute seat voted REJECT, outvoted) / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `cli_pane`: `Guard::Terminal => None`; cli::run `site = env.non_pane_locator(Cli)` is None for Ambiguous so the terminal fallback never fires; hook.rs ~4021 `Guard::Terminal => Some(HookOutcome::default())` with no diagnostic. The design's problem statement (lines 60-62) names exactly this leak as the motivating case and the Goal gives hc terminals full messaging; the \"binds nothing\" limit exists only in TRUST-POLICY.md:824, text the diff itself adds, and both confirming seats grepped the design and found no acceptance of it. Refute seat's point (TRUST-POLICY:824 accepted limit; design acceptance only says LocateCaller picks the root) is answered by the majority's provenance argument and the silent-outcome point.\n  fix-shape hint: let a Verified terminal bind as a Terminal site (CLI and hook), or at minimum emit a user-visible message in the verified-but-unbound case so the limit is not silent."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 11, "key": "[Should-fix] scripts/wake-e2e.py:2767", "text": "[Should-fix] scripts/wake-e2e.py:2767 — The tmux-named wake-e2e case counts wake lines over the whole cumulative screen with no per-case baseline, so the p1 control, which runs after the p0 case on the same recipient, sees the p0 case's stale wake line and reports a wake the daemon never typed for it. [lanes: test-causality-lifecycle]\n  verdict: confirmed (reproduce ✓ / refute ✗-survived / ground ✓)\n  evidence: `wake_lines = wake_count(self.screen(1000))` with `typed = wake_lines == (1 if expect else 0)`; `screen()` is `capture-pane -p -J -S -1000` (scrollback included); TMUX_CASES = (send-p0, send-p1) run in order on one pane with no history clear; the same file baselines elsewhere (`wakes_before` ~2285, `calls_before` in this case). Not run live (final review records the tmux-named cases were not run).\n  fix-shape hint: take `wakes_before = wake_count(self.screen(1000))` before the send and compare the delta."}, {"section": "Confirmed findings", "position": 12, "key": "[Should-fix] src/store/local_seat_status.rs:92", "text": "[Should-fix] src/store/local_seat_status.rs:92 — The daemon-side rule that stops a resolved seat from taking a reused target's labels (`labels_of`'s `TargetLabels::fits` filter) is pinned by no test: removing it passes the whole suite, and the CLI test that used to pin this behaviour was deleted in this diff. ## ht-kqrt.55 (H3c): terminal.run, L1/L3 readings

Read-only readings of herdr-core (`/var/home/alepar/AleCode/herdr-core`, no live core run):

- L3, `crates/herdr-core/src/api/run.rs` `start`: `notify: !p.no_notify`, so `no_notify: true` makes the record carry
  `notify == false`. `die_with_parent: parent.is_some() && !p.outlive_parent`, so `outlive_parent: true` keeps the
  child alive with its parent. An explicit `parent` param wins over `no_parent` (`(Some(parent), _) => ...`); `no_parent`
  only suppresses the caller-derived parent. So `NewTerminal` sends `parent = <caller terminal>` explicitly and
  `NewTab` sends no `parent`; both send `no_parent: true`.
- L3, `crates/herdr-core/src/notes.rs`: a note about a child is typed into its parent only `if t.notify` (`Job::Notes` /
  `Job::NotesAfar` are queued under `t.notify`; `unseen` also filters `t.notify`). With `notify == false` nothing is
  typed into the parent.
- `keep: false`: `Registry::finish` makes any finish final for a non-kept terminal; on `spawn_failed` of a non-kept
  record `start` removes the record (`core.remove`), so a `spawn_failed` outcome usually leaves no record but is still
  `OutcomeUnknown` per the spec (a kept or timed-out record may remain).
- L2 (exec keeps the pid) is not exercised here: the gate shim lands with `launch-gate`.

Remaining evidence (live, not run): L1 (an unknown `herdr.tab` id makes a new app tab; herdr-app is macOS only) and the
live L3 check against a real core (`hc run` with the five flags, then no `<hc>` note typed into a parent agent). Both
need a real core or the desktop app.

Open seam point for the next hc leaf: `CreatedTarget::validate` (ports.rs) accepts an hc witness only with
`Placement::NewTerminal`; `HcHost::create_target` returns a `NewTab` result with the hc witness but does not call
`validate()` on it, because the seam test `created_target_validate_per_witness_kind` pins `HcCore` + `NewTab` as invalid.

## hc-smoke (ht-kqrt.52)

Branch ran: the real-core smoke ships. `https://releases.herdr.dev/core/0.1.15/manifest.json` was fetched; both Linux
tarballs were downloaded and match the manifest sha256 (x86_64 `efbb1382...8d66`, aarch64 `e4939faa...a4f`); both rows are
in `tests/herdr-core-releases.tsv`, no macOS or Windows rows. `scripts/hc-smoke` (cache
`~/.cache/herdr-threads/herdr-core-releases/0.1.15/`, sha256 checked every run) passed locally against private cores
(`ht-smoke-<uuid>`, `env -i`, scratch XDG, `hc run --bg` only, `server.stop` at the end; no core process left,
`scripts/check-no-leaked-processes` shows none of this task's). Output: `evidence/ht-kqrt.52/hc-smoke.out`.

Live results: probe (hello 0.1.15, features, methods); observation (snapshot seq, `created`/`finished` events with a
higher seq); labels (at run and by `terminal.label`); D10 rows (`keep:false` exit 3 is `finished/exited`; `keep:true`
across `server.stop` + restart is `live`, no pid, `stopped_by_restart`, same `tN`); attach and probe through the real
daemon (`daemon ensure` with `--host-endpoint` the core socket: `daemon health` shows the hc host reachability ready,
coherent_enumeration supported); K5 (a daemon autostarted inside a terminal has no `HC_*` variable, is no descendant of
the terminal and survives `terminal.kill`, while a plain background child dies); L2 (`sh -c 'echo $$; exec sleep'`:
`terminal.pid` equals the exec'd process, still live); L3 (control child with default flags typed an `<hc>` note into a
reporting parent; an `outlive_parent` + `no_notify` child typed nothing). L2 and L3 are live here, which settles the
fallbacks above for those two rows.

Not driven live: HcHost is exercised through the daemon (attach, probe) and the wire the daemon reads, not by Rust unit
calls; the shell revival of a `stopped_by_restart` terminal through `hc attach` (a pty attach did not revive it within
3 s; only the unchanged address and record are asserted); L1 stays macOS-only remaining evidence. Core id rotation
across restart was seen in one probe but not in the smoke run, so it is not asserted.

## L2 (ht-kqrt.54)

Read-only reading of herdr-core (`/var/home/alepar/AleCode/herdr-core/crates/herdr-core`, no live core run):

- `src/holder.rs` `spawn`: the holder starts the terminal's argv through `platform::spawn_pty` and records `pid = child.id()`
  of that one spawned child; it reports it as `SPAWNED{pid}`. `src/runtime.rs` stores it as the record's `t.pid = Some(pid)`.
  `src/api/run.rs` `start` hands `argv` (the gate shim) to that spawn and records nothing else about the process.
- An `exec` replaces the process image and keeps its pid, so `terminal.pid` is the pid of the harness after the shim `exec`s.
  Nothing in `run.rs` or the holder spawns a second process for the argv or re-reads the pid.
- Test (fallback, no real core): `exec_keeps_pid` in `tests/cli/launch_gate.rs` records the shim's pid before `run_with`
  and the exec'd stand-in's `$$`; they are equal.
- Live L2 (a real core's `terminal.pid` before and after the shim) stays remaining evidence.
- The shim keeps `HC_SESSION` too: the spec's rule is the K5 list minus `host::hc::ENV_VARS`, which includes it.

## hc handoff CLI and launch gate (ht-kqrt.57, H3d2)

Spec deviation, recorded as the bead asks: `launch` gains no creation flags. `LaunchArgs` is a pane selector and a launch
has no bootstrap for the gate to authorize, so the spec's `handoff`/`launch --space` line is narrowed to `handoff`;
in-place refusal (`launch --pane tN` into a herdr-core terminal, answered by `native_launch_capability() ==
Unsupported`) is `launch`'s whole hc surface. `handoff --space SPACE --new-tab LABEL` and `handoff --new-terminal`
select the herdr-core host in `cli::hc_target::select`: a `Terminal` caller site names its own core; a plain shell with no
Herdr context uses `HcDiscovery` (exactly one core, else a refusal naming what was found; `hc` is never run). A Herdr pane
caller or any Herdr context keeps the Herdr path, and `--new-terminal` there is refused. `--thread` and `--new-thread`
into a hc target are refused before any creation (no `terminal.run`). The daemon's `AuthorizeLaunchGate` handler is
`service::launch_gate::authorize`, advertised as `launch.gate_v1`. `topology_runtime::run`'s V3 arms (first run and
retry) are `run_target`; a retried V3 record rebuilds its host from the frozen namespace endpoint.

- L1 (unknown `herdr.tab` makes a new app tab): answered against fake_hc only. `space_label_set` shows `--space w1` sends
  exactly `{herdr.space=w1, herdr.tab=<fresh id>, ht.bootstrap, ht.label}`; whether a real herdr-app creates a tab from
  an unknown `herdr.tab` is macOS-only and stays remaining evidence.
- L2 / L3: unchanged from the entries above and the hc-smoke entry (read-only reading of `run.rs`/`holder.rs`; live
  smoke for pid-after-exec and for `outlive_parent` + `no_notify` stopping every `<hc>` note).
- L4: the socket path comes from herdr-core `src/paths.rs` (`Paths::from_env`): `HC_SOCKET`, else `socket` in
  `config.toml`, else `<runtime dir>/<prefix><session>.sock`; `HcDiscovery` derives the same path. Codex `--no-daemon`
  for `HostKind::Hc` is `host_launch_argv` (ht-kqrt.54); the live Codex run stays remaining evidence.

## N phase gate (ht-kqrt.34)

Tested source: task branch `task-ht-kqrt.34` on the stack base `f1ae3da79` (integration tip plus ht-kqrt.135); source tree
`f19dc4c28b27ade8b5566a830702901c1e690c28`. Suite: `tests/named_seats.rs` (own `[[test]]`, nextest group `named-seats`).

Commands and results (heavy wrapper, `nice`, `TMPDIR=/tmp/sp-503081fe/ht-kqrt.34`, Herdr 0.9.5 isolated sessions):

- `cargo test --locked --all-features --test named_seats`: 7 passed.
- `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`: clean. `scripts/check-harness-neutral`: ok, no new entry.
  `scripts/check-wire-compat`: ok, no baseline line. `nice scripts/check-default-features`: exit 0.
- `git diff f1ae3da79 -- tests/golden_surfaces.rs tests/fixtures/golden`: empty (Herdr-pane surfaces unchanged).
- Unfiltered `cargo nextest run --locked --all-targets --all-features` with `HT_LEAK_RUN_ID` exported: 8613 run, 8567 passed, 46 failed,
  53 skipped. 333 s of test time, 454 s wall including the build, on a CPU shared with ML training: over the 300 s budget, which
  release-not-blocked-on-suite-time covers. All seven `named_seats` tests, `host_neutral_scan` (ht-kqrt.65) and the multinode named
  tests passed. `scripts/check-no-leaked-processes --run-id` after the run: no leaked processes (no temp ledger).
- The 46 failures were rerun at the base commit `f1ae3da79` (throwaway worktree, removed): the same 46 fail there (0 passed), so none
  is introduced by this task. Causes seen: no `zsh` on this host (`*_through_zsh`, `rendered_actions_execute_as_shell_commands`,
  `action_formatter_round_trips_unicode_separators_through_bash_and_zsh`), Herdr restart/restore tests timing out at 31 s
  (`trust_policy::*`, `*_restart_keeps_seats_in_restore_hold`), and others listed in `evidence/ht-kqrt.34/failed.txt`.

Defect found and fixed by the recovery round trip: a named caller's `retry` of a completed automatic handoff built its namespace from
the reserved named locator instead of the Herdr endpoint the handoff was recorded under (`automatic current namespace mismatch`).
`topology_runtime::namespace` now resolves the Herdr endpoint lazily for a named caller, as the handoff does.

### Spikes (named half) and E1

- S1 allocation vs enumeration race and restart `CarryForward`: `identity::reconcile::s1_named_allocation_race_and_carry_forward`
  (tests/identity/reconcile.rs).
- S2 first empty named publication clears holds: `store::named_presence::s2_first_empty_named_publication_clears_holds`.
- S3 `watch` never stops on `no_pane` for a Named site: `cli::watch::named_site_watch_never_stops_on_no_pane`; the mod's spawn env

### Regressions found by the first run and their fixes (root cause, one commit per group)

1. zsh (3): environmental, see above.
2. Hook contract (3, ht-kqrt.70): production is right (7 installed Claude events, `Stop` and `StopFailure` decode). `installed_argv_and_hook_parser_are_one_contract` now expects 7; the two "unsupported payload" tests used `Stop` as their undeclared event and now use `Notification`. Commit 82ccaea4d.
3. `no_wake_reports` (migration 0012) is a passive once-per-episode mark written by the sender-side `wake_status` read; classified in `KNOWN_UNMAPPED` of `service::kicks`. Commit 82ccaea4d.
4. Unowned test spawns (`tests/host/named_tmux.rs`, `tests/installer_integrations.rs`) moved to `test_support::spawn`. Commit 82ccaea4d.
5. Herdr-pane wake (about 17, the serious group): not a production wake regression. `LaneProbe::registered_idle_events`, `set_registered_idle_hook`, `set_registered_wake_hook` and `registered_attempts` followed `KickRegistry::pacer`, the first registered Pacer, which is the built-in named host's lane since ht-kqrt.69 (it attaches first); the rigs watched the wrong lane. They now follow a dialled host's lane through `registered_pacer` (matching `first_host_pacer`, dialled-only). All `trust_policy::*` also queried `herdr_hosts` with no kind filter and read the named row; scoped to `kind='herdr'`. Herdr-release-smoke 0.9.5 and `lanes_latency` agree the production path is intact. Commit 7c0999787.
6. Host-count and host-id expectations (the named row is intended, design lines 352-354): `config_*`, `pane_close_retirement`, `session_bindings_smoke` and `remote_addresses` fixtures count or pick the Herdr row (`kind='herdr'`). `remote_addresses_resolve_on_their_home_node` was a real source defect: `RemoteResolver::answer` reads every host (G1) and the topology-less named host's refusal counted as an unreadable host, turning every name into `host_unavailable`; hosts with no pane topology now answer through the new defaulted `HostPort::reads_live_topology` (false for the named host) and are skipped (a host-kind name would trip the host-neutrality scan). `node_layout::second_claim_host_attaches_lazily`: since H1b (ht-kqrt.44) an unclassifiable server is refused before a row is written; the test's `SilentServer` now answers the kind probe's `ping`. `peer_launch_terminal`: a new terminal sends no labels (design: `--new-terminal` has no app labels; `new_terminal_no_app_labels_parent_caller` pins the host side), so the test asserts that instead of `ht.label`. Commit 75eddac3e, a5f9c3a09.
7. Composition (5): the test binding insert and a join matched `herdr_hosts` on `node_id` (now two rows): joined on `host_id`. `elected_shutdown_joins_idle_monitor...` held every `idle-exit` clock sample; the monitor now samples more than once, so only the first is held. `store_only_daemon_serves_without_host_workers` asserts the built-in host's one wake and one observation lane and no archival lane; the built-in observation lane could log `lane observation: Cancelled` on shutdown, so the lane error log now skips a cancelled pass (4d86d123f). Commit 2e8104f2e.
8. `wire_compat`: the pane-name source opened a second client and asked `capabilities` again; `render_history` now hands its answer over (`PaneNameSource::known_capabilities`, `LocalSocketClient::seed_capabilities`). Commit 109aa6f17.
9. `detached_output_survives_ensure_parent...`: ht-kqrt.73 made `spawn_detached` run `internal daemon-detach`, whose stdout carries the pid; the test launcher exec'd the fixture as that hop, so libtest's header on the piped stdout hit EPIPE. The launcher now plays the hop (starts the fixture off its stdout, prints its pid). It failed identically on the .30 binary and passed on main. Commit ef6cdd2bd.

| Handoff into an hc terminal (gate shim, `(core, tN)`, bootstrap Send) | `hc_handoff::handoff_into_fake_hc_runs_gate_shim`, `launch_gate_*`, `launched_worker_exit_retires_seat` | no |

### Unwired-interface sweep

`HostPort` answers (production reader): `kind` (service/host_sessions.rs), `wake_capability` (notification/dispatch.rs, service/host_sessions.rs), `target_wake_capability` (service/dispatch.rs), `target_root_process` (service/locate.rs, peer_launcher.rs), `server_process` (service/locate.rs), `archival_evidence` (service/archival.rs), `closure_authority` (identity/reconcile.rs, identity/repair.rs): all consulted.

`CallerSite` answers: `moved_target` (cli/mod.rs), `relative_pane` (cli/caller_guard.rs), `sent_scope`, `required_capability`, `locate_candidate`, `terminal_address`, `claim_host_locator`, `env_assignments`: consulted. `person_origin_allowed`, `enrolls_on_first_use`, `attempts_continuity`, `host_scoped_lookup` and `cache_key`: UNUSED in production (only `tests/cli/caller_location.rs` calls them; production matches on `CallerLocator` variants, and `sent_scope()` replaced `host_scoped_lookup()`). Not wired inline: wiring `person_origin_allowed` changes the `human me init` behaviour for a Terminal site. Filed as blocker bead ht-tyil.

NodeDelta 0012 columns: `herdr_hosts.kind` (store/apply.rs, store/control/retirement.rs, service/host_sessions.rs), `seats.name` (store/named_seats.rs, store/seat_directory.rs, store/queries.rs), `occupant_bindings.readonly` (service/delivery_pull.rs, store/receipts.rs, store/seats/cooperative.rs), `locator_hint` (store/queries.rs inspect), `no_wake_reports` (store/no_wake_reports.rs, service/dispatch.rs): all read by a production path.

### Findings

- Gap test added: daemon-level tmux composer wake (p0 types the wake marker into an idle composer, p1 at T = 0 does not, sender sees no `wake=no_wake`). Passed on first correct rig; the one rig error was the test's (an `exec`ed pane shell leaves the binding no distinct pane process; fixed by keeping the shell as the pane process, as `config_smoke` does).
- Filed ht-tyil for the five unused `CallerSite` answers.
